Read this first — honesty limits.

  • Maturity labels are plain-words labels from the universal research maturity scale (E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational; T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative; U Unknown). A label says how mature a technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.
  • Any dates, horizons, or timelines here are PLANNING WINDOWS, never predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs.
  • Public records are documented evidence, never established fact. Signature records are design-study, never presented as authentic public record.
  • No consciousness or sentience claim is made here. Machine consciousness is theories and testable models only, never an established fact; no claim is made that a machine is conscious, sentient, or aware.
  • Climate restoration records are research only; no proven-intervention claims are made.
  • Post-quantum records are defensive security only.
  • Synthetic biology is design-study only, with biosafety screening; no pathogen, toxin, gene-drive, or synthesis-enabling content appears here.

This website is the subject website for the Advanced Energy Storage Database database, public edition. It holds the full database: all 24 records.

All 24 record titles: What Advanced Energy Storage Is; Lithium-Ion Batteries at Scale; Solid-State Batteries; Flow Batteries for Long Storage; Sodium-Ion Batteries; Grid Energy Storage; Home Battery Systems; Supercapacitors and Fast Storage; Compressed Air Energy Storage; Pumped Hydro Storage; Thermal Energy Storage; Hydrogen as Stored Energy; Battery Chemistry and Materials; Battery Management Systems; Battery Safety and Thermal Control; Battery Recycling and Second Life; Measuring Storage: Capacity and Efficiency; Storage for Solar and Wind Power; Storage for Electric Vehicles; Microgrids and Local Storage; Long-Duration Storage; Storage Costs and Lifetime; Storage Standards and Testing; The Advanced Energy Storage Record Itself.

1. Descriptions

Terms in Plain Words

database: a database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to. record: a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. slug: a slug is the short web-address name of a database, written with lower-case words joined by hyphens. manifest: the manifest is the master list of all databases in the network, with each database's slug, record kind, identifier prefix, and record count. record kind: a record kind is the label that says what sort of entry a record is, for example a future advanced energy storage record. identifier prefix: an identifier prefix is the fixed letters at the start of every record identifier in one database, for example JAH-ADES-. generator: a generator is a program that creates new records or files on demand from a starting number called a seed, instead of storing every output in advance. seed: a seed is a starting number fed to a generator so that the same seed always produces the same output. generator gate: a generator gate is a check a generator must pass before its output is accepted, for example the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind. JCC code: a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, is a short code that points to one exact record, written as JAHDB followed by the database slug, a hash sign, and the record identifier. API: an API, which stands for Application Programming Interface, is a defined way for one program to ask another program for data or work. MCP: an MCP, which stands for Model Context Protocol, is a plug that lets an AI (artificial intelligence) program read a database directly. AI: AI stands for artificial intelligence, which is a program that performs tasks that usually need human thought, such as answering, classifying, or generating. SQLite: SQLite is a small database engine that stores a whole database in one file on disk. SQL: SQL, which stands for Structured Query Language, is the language used to ask questions of a relational database. JSON: JSON, which stands for JavaScript Object Notation, is a plain-text format for storing structured data with named fields. HTML: HTML, which stands for HyperText Markup Language, is the language that marks up the structure of a web page. CSS: CSS, which stands for Cascading Style Sheets, is the language that describes how a web page looks. URL: a URL, which stands for Uniform Resource Locator, is the full web address of a page or file. HTTP: HTTP, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol, is the set of rules a browser uses to fetch a page from a web server. HTTPS: HTTPS, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol Secure, is HTTP sent over a protected connection. NIST: NIST stands for the National Institute of Standards and Technology, the United States measurement and standards agency that runs the post-quantum cryptography standardization project. QKD: QKD stands for Quantum Key Distribution, a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that eavesdropping can be detected. PQC: PQC stands for Post-Quantum Cryptography, encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. DOE: DOE stands for the Department of Energy, the United States government department for energy research and policy. test suite: a test suite is a collection of automated tests that check whether a program behaves as specified. demo: a demo, short for demonstration, is a small working program that runs and shows its result. website interface: a website interface is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses a system. JAH: JAH is the initials of Justin Addam Higgins, the maker name used across the network. design-study: a design-study is a Signature-made design, an invented proposal that is labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. UNREACHABLE: UNREACHABLE is the label recorded for a resource whose fetch failed, together with the exact reason the fetch returned. maturity label: a maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is: E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational, T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative, and U Unknown. research question: a research question is the single clear question a research project sets out to answer, written before any work begins. prototype: a prototype is an early working model built to test whether an idea works in practice, before any finished product is made. simulation: a simulation is a computer model that imitates a real system so its behaviour can be tested without building it. validation: validation is the work of checking, by running tests and comparing against evidence, that a design does what its specification says it does. evidence label: an evidence label says what kind of evidence a record rests on: documented means built from fetched public records, and design-study means a Signature-made design. battery: a battery is a device that stores energy as chemical energy and gives it back as electricity when it is used. anode: an anode is the part of a battery where electric current flows out into the circuit when the battery is giving power. cathode: a cathode is the part of a battery where electric current flows in from the circuit when the battery is giving power. electrolyte: an electrolyte is the material inside a battery that lets charged particles move between the anode and the cathode while keeping the two apart. energy density: energy density is how much energy a storage device holds for its weight or its size, usually stated in watt-hours per kilogram. watt-hour: a watt-hour is a unit of energy: one watt of power used for one hour, and a kilowatt-hour is one thousand watt-hours. charge cycle: a charge cycle is one full charge and one full discharge of a battery, and a battery is rated for how many cycles it can survive before it wears out. round-trip efficiency: round-trip efficiency is the share of the energy put into a storage device that can be got back out again, stated as a percentage. grid: the grid is the network of wires, power stations, and substations that carries electricity from where it is made to where it is used. solid-state battery: a solid-state battery is a battery whose electrolyte is a solid material instead of a liquid, which can make it safer and able to hold more energy. flow battery: a flow battery is a battery that stores its energy in liquid chemicals held in tanks, so its capacity grows by using bigger tanks. supercapacitor: a supercapacitor is a storage device that holds electric charge directly on surfaces, charging and discharging very fast but holding less energy than a battery of the same size. lithium-ion: lithium-ion means a battery type that moves lithium ions, which are charged lithium atoms, through its electrolyte to store and release energy. state of charge: state of charge is how full a battery is, stated as a percentage from empty to full. thermal runaway: thermal runaway is a failure in which a battery heats itself faster than it can cool, which can lead to fire, and it is the main safety failure battery designs must prevent. photosynthesis: photosynthesis is the process green plants use to turn sunlight, water, and carbon dioxide into sugars and oxygen. artificial photosynthesis: artificial photosynthesis is a human-made system that copies the plant process, using sunlight to split water or to turn carbon dioxide into a fuel. photocatalyst: a photocatalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change when light falls on it, without being used up itself. catalyst: a catalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change without being used up itself. water splitting: water splitting is the chemical change that breaks water into hydrogen and oxygen using energy, here energy from sunlight. hydrogen: hydrogen is the lightest chemical element, a gas that can be burned or used in a fuel cell to release energy, leaving water behind. oxygen: oxygen is a gas in the air that living things breathe and that is released when water is split. carbon dioxide: carbon dioxide is a gas in the air, made when fuels burn, that artificial photosynthesis can turn into a fuel instead of letting it build up. solar fuel: a solar fuel is a fuel made using sunlight, such as hydrogen made by splitting water with sunlight, that can be stored and used later. photoelectrochemical cell: a photoelectrochemical cell is a device that uses light falling on a material in a liquid to drive a chemical change, such as splitting water. semiconductor: a semiconductor is a material that can absorb light and turn it into moving electric charges, and it is the light-catching part of most solar devices. light absorber: a light absorber is the part of a device that catches sunlight and turns it into electric charges that can do chemical work. electron: an electron is a tiny particle with a negative electric charge, and moving electrons are what electric current is made of. proton: a proton is a tiny particle with a positive electric charge found in the nucleus, which is the centre, of an atom. artificial leaf: an artificial leaf is a thin device shaped like a leaf that uses sunlight to split water into hydrogen and oxygen. qubit: a qubit, short for quantum bit, is the basic unit of quantum information, like a bit in an ordinary computer but able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. quantum bit: a quantum bit is the same as a qubit: the basic unit of quantum information, able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. superposition: superposition is the quantum property by which a qubit can hold a blend of zero and one at the same time until it is measured. entanglement: entanglement is the quantum property by which two qubits can be linked so that measuring one at once fixes the state of the other, however far apart they are. decoherence: decoherence is the loss of a quantum state when the qubit is disturbed by its surroundings, such as heat or stray electric fields. quantum error correction: quantum error correction is a set of methods that spread one qubit of information across many physical qubits so that errors can be found and fixed. quantum key distribution: quantum key distribution is a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that anyone listening in can be detected. quantum network: a quantum network is a set of connected quantum devices that can share entanglement and send quantum information between distant places. quantum computer: a quantum computer is a computer that stores and processes information in qubits and uses quantum rules such as superposition and entanglement. quantum gate: a quantum gate is a basic operation that changes the state of one or more qubits, like a step in a quantum calculation. measurement: measurement is the act of reading a qubit, which forces its blended state to settle into a definite zero or one. post-quantum cryptography: post-quantum cryptography is encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. quantum repeater: a quantum repeater is a device that extends a quantum network over long distances by refreshing entanglement partway along the path. quantum sensing: quantum sensing is measuring with quantum devices, such as using qubits to detect tiny magnetic fields or tiny changes in time.

What Advanced Energy Storage Is

JAH-ADES-000001 · advanced energy storage identity · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is What Advanced Energy Storage Is. Advanced energy storage is the set of technologies that hold energy made at one time so it can be used at a later time, going beyond the batteries in everyday use today. It covers batteries with new chemistries, storage that holds energy as moving air, as water held high, as heat, or as hydrogen gas, and the systems that manage all of them. Storage matters because sunlight and wind come and go, while people need power on demand, so the gap between making energy and using it must be bridged by something that holds it safely. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The system identity is fixed: this is a future advanced energy storage record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: How can energy made at one time be held safely and returned later, at the scale a whole grid needs? Lithium-Ion Batteries at Scale

JAH-ADES-000002 · lithium-ion batteries at scale · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Lithium-Ion Batteries at Scale. A lithium-ion battery is a rechargeable battery that moves lithium ions, which are charged lithium atoms, through its electrolyte to store and release energy. At small scale it powers phones and laptop computers; at large scale, thousands of cells are joined into packs that store power for homes, for vehicles, and for the grid. This record specifies how lithium-ion storage scales up: cell design, pack assembly, cooling, management electronics, and the testing that proves a large pack behaves like its cells promised. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Solid-State Batteries

JAH-ADES-000003 · solid-state batteries · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Solid-State Batteries. A solid-state battery is a battery whose electrolyte is a solid material instead of a liquid. A solid electrolyte can make the battery safer, because there is no flammable liquid to leak, and it can allow a denser anode, which is the part where current flows out, so the battery holds more energy for its size. The research problem is the boundary where the solid meets the electrodes: the materials must stay in close contact through thousands of charge cycles without cracking or losing their connection. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. Flow Batteries for Long Storage

JAH-ADES-000004 · flow batteries for long storage · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Flow Batteries for Long Storage. A flow battery is a battery that stores its energy in liquid chemicals held in tanks, and pumps those liquids past electrodes to charge and discharge. Its power depends on the size of the electrodes, and its capacity depends on the size of the tanks, so the two can be sized separately — a useful property when storage must last many hours. This record specifies flow battery research: the chemistries, the membranes that separate the liquids, the pumps, and the cost of storing energy for eight hours or more. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Sodium-Ion Batteries

JAH-ADES-000005 · sodium-ion batteries · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Sodium-Ion Batteries. A sodium-ion battery is a rechargeable battery that moves sodium ions, which are charged sodium atoms, instead of lithium ions. Sodium is abundant and cheap, found in ordinary salt, so sodium-ion batteries could lower the cost of storage where weight matters less than price, such as grid storage and home storage. The research question is how close sodium-ion performance can come to lithium-ion: its energy density is lower today, and its cycle life must be proven over thousands of charges. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. Grid Energy Storage

JAH-ADES-000006 · grid energy storage · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Grid Energy Storage. Grid energy storage is storage connected to the grid, which is the network of wires, power stations, and substations that carries electricity. It steadies the grid by absorbing power when supply is high and releasing it when demand is high, by holding frequency steady, and by keeping power on through short outages. This record specifies grid storage research: sizing, siting, control, response time, and the rules that let storage earn its keep by the services it provides to the grid. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. Home Battery Systems

JAH-ADES-000007 · home battery systems · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Home Battery Systems. A home battery system is a battery pack installed in a home, usually beside rooftop solar panels, that stores sunshine made in the day for use in the evening. It can keep lights on through an outage, cut the home's peak demand from the grid, and let the household use more of its own solar power instead of sending it away. This record specifies home storage research: sizing for a household, safe installation, the inverter that turns battery power into household power, and the controls that decide when to charge and discharge. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. Supercapacitors and Fast Storage

JAH-ADES-000008 · supercapacitors and fast storage · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Supercapacitors and Fast Storage. A supercapacitor is a storage device that holds electric charge directly on surfaces, charging and discharging very fast but holding less energy than a battery of the same size. Fast storage matters where power is needed in seconds — to catch a braking vehicle, to steady a grid frequency dip, or to bridge the gap until a battery takes over. This record specifies fast storage research: electrode materials with huge surface area, the balance between energy and power, and pairing supercapacitors with batteries so each does what it does best. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The system identity is fixed: this is a future advanced energy storage record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The research subject covered here is Compressed Air Energy Storage. Compressed air energy storage holds energy by pumping air into a sealed cavern or tank until it is under high pressure, and releases it by letting the air out through a turbine that makes electricity. It suits very large, long storage where the geology allows it, because the storage vessel is the earth itself. The research subject covered here is Pumped Hydro Storage. Pumped hydro storage holds energy by pumping water uphill into a reservoir when power is plentiful, and releases it by letting the water run back down through turbines when power is needed. It is the oldest and largest form of grid storage, proven over a century, and still the benchmark every new storage technology is measured against. This record specifies the research: new sites with smaller footprints, closed-loop systems that do not dam rivers, faster response, and the environmental review each site must pass. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. The research subject covered here is Thermal Energy Storage. Thermal energy storage holds energy as heat or cold: hot water in insulated tanks, molten salt held hot through the night, ice made at night to cool a building by day, or materials that melt and freeze at a chosen temperature. Heat is the largest single use of energy in buildings and industry, so storing heat directly can be cheaper than storing electricity and turning it back into heat. The research subject covered here is Hydrogen as Stored Energy. Hydrogen is the lightest chemical element, a gas that can be burned or used in a fuel cell to release energy, leaving water behind. As storage, hydrogen is made with surplus electricity, kept in tanks or caverns, and used later for power, for heat, or as a chemical feedstock. Its strength is long storage and transport: hydrogen can be held for months and moved by pipe or ship. Its weakness is the energy lost at each step of making, storing, and using it, which this record measures honestly. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. The research subject covered here is Battery Chemistry and Materials. Battery chemistry is the choice of materials for the anode, the cathode, and the electrolyte, and that choice decides almost everything: how much energy the battery holds, how fast it charges, how long it lasts, how safe it is, and what it costs. New chemistries are tested in small cells first, measured against the same yardsticks, and only the best move to larger trials. This record specifies the research: material discovery, cell testing, ageing studies, and the standards that let one chemistry be compared fairly with another. The research subject covered here is Battery Management Systems. A battery management system is the electronics and software that watch every cell in a pack: its voltage, its temperature, and its state of charge, which is how full it is. It balances the cells so none is overcharged or over-discharged, it cuts power if anything goes wrong, and it reports the pack's health so owners know when it is ageing. The research subject covered here is Battery Safety and Thermal Control. Battery safety is the work of making sure a battery never harms people or property, and its hardest failure is thermal runaway, a failure in which a battery heats itself faster than it can cool, which can lead to fire. Safety research covers the materials that resist catching fire, the design that keeps one failing cell from setting off its neighbours, the cooling that carries heat away, and the tests that prove a pack survives abuse without spreading fire. The research subject covered here is Measuring Storage: Capacity and Efficiency. Measuring storage means stating, in the same units everywhere, how much energy a device holds, how fast it can deliver it, how much is lost in a round trip, and how long it lasts. Without shared measures, one maker's claim cannot be compared with another's, and buyers cannot tell a good battery from a good advertisement. This record specifies the measurement standard: capacity in watt-hours, power in watts, round-trip efficiency as a percentage, cycle life as counted cycles, and the test conditions under which each number was measured. The research subject covered here is Microgrids and Local Storage. A microgrid is a small local grid — a campus, a village, a hospital, or an island — that can make its own power, store it, and run on its own when the main grid fails. Local storage is what makes a microgrid work: it holds the local solar power, steadies the local frequency, and keeps the lights on through an outage. Long-Duration Storage

JAH-ADES-000021 · long-duration storage · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Long-Duration Storage. Long-duration storage is storage that can deliver power for many hours or days, bridging the gap between a windy week and a calm week, or between summer sunshine and winter demand. Short storage covers hours; long storage covers seasons. The candidates are flow batteries with large tanks, compressed air in caverns, pumped water, hydrogen, and heat. This record specifies the research: the cost per unit of energy stored, which matters more than power for long storage, and the losses over weeks of holding energy. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The system identity is fixed: this is a future advanced energy storage record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: Which long-duration technologies hold energy for weeks at a cost grids can afford? Storage Costs and Lifetime

JAH-ADES-000022 · storage costs and lifetime · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Advanced Energy Storage Database, catalog item 19 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Storage Costs and Lifetime. Storage costs and lifetime decide whether a storage system is worth building: the price of buying it, the price of running it, the energy it delivers over its life, and the cost of replacing it when it wears out. A cheap battery that dies young can cost more over its life than a dear battery that lasts. This record specifies the research: lifetime cost accounting, ageing models that predict how capacity fades, warranties that state what is promised, and the cost curves that show how prices fall as production grows. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Storage Standards and Testing. Storage standards and testing are the shared rules that prove a storage system does what its maker claims: safety tests that abuse a battery on purpose, performance tests that measure capacity and efficiency under stated conditions, and lifetime tests that cycle a battery thousands of times. Standards let buyers compare, insurers price risk, and regulators approve. This record specifies the research: test design, the conditions under which numbers are measured, and the certification that records a pass. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. 3. Science Proof

What Advanced Energy Storage Is

JAH-ADES-000001 · advanced energy storage identity · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Science proof: The science proof rests on established practice and documented research, not on a new discovery. Organized knowledge, structured storage, indexed search, and automated testing are the standard methods of software engineering and information retrieval, which is the study of how to find the right records among many. The subject science itself — the physics, chemistry, and engineering behind What Advanced Energy Storage Is — is documented in the sources cited in this record, and this record states clearly where that science is settled and where it is still open research. A specification that names its parts, a database that stores its records in one shape, a search that returns records with their provenance, a generator that is seeded so its output is repeatable, and a test suite that checks behavior by running it are each established practice, and this system composes them without claiming a new law of nature. Nothing in this record claims a result that was not computed, and nothing here claims that a design is a finished deployed system. The maturity label E3 Engineering-ready is the honest statement of how far the subject has actually come.

Scientific equation: validation = executed demo + executed program + round-trip code + fetched sources + passed generator gates + stated maturity

Proof sketch: The proof sketch follows the validation chain: the demo is executed and its output is captured, the program is executed and reports a deterministic match, the JCC code is recomputed from the record and compared, every cited source web address is fetched and must answer HTTP 200, the maturity label is checked against the pair's label, and the generator is run with a fixed seed twice and must produce identical output. Each link in the chain is observed, not assumed, and a failure in any link fails the record. The scientific claim is stated, not machine-checked, and the machine-checked part is the arithmetic of the demo figures.

Proof status: The arithmetic is machine-checked by the demo; the scientific claim is stated, not machine-checked.

History: The 100 Future-Level Resources catalog was pasted on 2026-11-17 as a future technology plan for the JAH Network Master AI (artificial intelligence), with the words of the paste kept straight as another AI's drafted plan, not the maker's own wording, and with the paste's own note that it is a forward-looking research catalog, not a claim that all of these technologies are achievable or commercially viable. The advanced energy storage type was item 19 in the catalog's priority order, kept in Wave 1 as an EXPAND pair. Later versions will record their changes here, with the date, the change, and the reason, and no version will ever silently rewrite an earlier version. 5. How It Works — Construction Workflow

What Advanced Energy Storage Is

JAH-ADES-000001 · advanced energy storage identity · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can energy made at one time be held safely and returned later, at the scale a whole grid needs? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Without storage, energy made when it is not needed is wasted, and energy needed when the sun is down or the wind is still cannot be supplied. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of What Advanced Energy Storage Is as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Storage, which is a device or system that holds energy for later use; Charge, which is the act of putting energy into storage; Discharge, which is the act of taking energy back out of storage; Capacity, which is how much energy a storage device can hold. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 250, round trip efficiency percent 90, cycle life 5000, lifetime years 15, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready. The demo figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 250, round trip efficiency percent 90, cycle life 5000, lifetime years 15, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Future expansion opportunities. The next work after this record is to deepen the neighbouring subjects it links to, to add records for the parts of the subject this record names but does not yet cover in full, and to re-fetch every source on a later date and record what changed, so the specification stays honest as the research moves.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to research, find, or check a future advanced energy storage record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. It connects to the other catalog types as follows: Fusion Energy Database (catalog item 18), whose power plants need storage to deliver steady power; Future Energy Systems Laboratory (catalog item 55), which tests new generation and storage together; Advanced Energy Networks Database (catalog item 35), which moves stored energy across planetary-scale grids. The connection rule from the catalog is that one type feeds the next: a database can supply a website, the website can use a generator, and the generator can be registered in the generator database, so the Master AI can discover and reuse all three in future projects. The catalog's convergence rule also applies: one research breakthrough should be usable by other divisions, so a material, a method, or a measurement from this subject is recorded in a form other subjects can reuse.

Verification path: To verify this record, re-read the specification requirements in the method and check each one against the record; run the demo and confirm its output matches the expected output exactly and reports Deterministic: MATCH; run the program and confirm it reports a deterministic match; recompute the JCC code from the record and confirm it round-trips; fetch every cited source web address and confirm it answers HTTP 200; confirm the maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready with its plain-words definition; run the pair generator with seed 42 twice and confirm identical output; and confirm the terms glossary defines every term the record uses in plain words.

Lithium-Ion Batteries at Scale

JAH-ADES-000002 · lithium-ion batteries at scale · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How do lithium-ion cells scale into packs that stay safe and keep their promised capacity for thousands of cycles? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A cell that works alone can fail when joined into a pack, because heat, ageing, and small differences between cells add up. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Lithium-Ion Batteries at Scale as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Cell, which is one single battery unit, the smallest piece that stores energy; Pack, which is many cells joined together with wiring, cooling, and controls; Management system, which is the electronics that watch every cell and keep it safe; Cooling, which is the removal of heat so cells stay in their safe range. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 260, round trip efficiency percent 92, cycle life 4000, lifetime years 12, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready. The demo figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 260, round trip efficiency percent 92, cycle life 4000, lifetime years 12, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Solid-State Batteries

JAH-ADES-000003 · solid-state batteries · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Can a solid electrolyte keep close contact with its electrodes through thousands of cycles without cracking? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Liquid electrolytes limit how much energy a battery can hold and how safe it can be, but solid replacements are hard to keep in contact. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. Grid Energy Storage

JAH-ADES-000006 · grid energy storage · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How should grid storage be sized, sited, and controlled to steady a grid fed by sunlight and wind? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A grid fed by sunlight and wind swings between surplus and shortage, and without storage those swings become outages. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Grid Energy Storage as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Frequency, which is how fast the grid's alternating current cycles, which must stay steady; Peak demand, which is the highest power use in a day, which storage can shave by discharging; Siting, which is choosing where a storage system is placed on the grid; Response time, which is how fast storage can start delivering power when asked. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 200, round trip efficiency percent 88, cycle life 6000, lifetime years 15, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. Proposed innovation. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Cavern, which is a sealed underground space that holds compressed air; Turbine, which is a machine with spinning blades that turns moving air into electricity; Heat of compression, which is the heat made when air is squeezed, which must be stored or it is lost; Geology, which is the rock and earth below ground, which decides where caverns can be sealed. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. Prototype or simulation plan. Working source code. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 100, round trip efficiency percent 45, cycle life 8000, lifetime years 20, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. Battery Chemistry and Materials

JAH-ADES-000013 · battery chemistry and materials · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which new battery materials survive thousands of cycles without losing capacity or safety? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Every better battery starts as a better material, and materials must be proven in cells before they can be trusted in packs. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.
