Battery Recycling and Second Life

JAH-ADES-000016 · battery recycling and second life · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can used batteries be graded safely for second life and their materials recovered at high rates? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A battery that is thrown away wastes its metals and its remaining energy, and a battery reused blindly can fail dangerously. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Battery Recycling and Second Life as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Recovery rate, which is the share of a battery's valuable material that recycling gets back; Grading, which is testing a used battery to decide what second job it can still do; Dismantling, which is taking a battery pack apart safely for reuse or recycling; Tracking, which is following a battery's life from manufacture to recycling. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 200, round trip efficiency percent 88, cycle life 3000, lifetime years 12, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Storage for Electric Vehicles as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Range, which is how far a vehicle can drive on one charge; Fast charging, which is filling a battery quickly without damaging it; Crash safety, which is keeping a battery safe when its vehicle is struck; Charging network, which is the public chargers a vehicle can use away from home. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 300, round trip efficiency percent 90, cycle life 3000, lifetime years 10, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready. The demo figures for this record are energy density wh per kg 300, round trip efficiency percent 90, cycle life 3000, lifetime years 10, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Storage Costs and Lifetime

JAH-ADES-000022 · storage costs and lifetime · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. A storage system is bought once and paid for over its whole life, so its lifetime cost, not its purchase price, is the honest number. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. Storage Standards and Testing

JAH-ADES-000023 · storage standards and testing · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which shared tests prove a storage system safe and true to its claimed capacity and efficiency? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Claims without shared tests are advertisements, and only a test that any laboratory can repeat can settle a claim. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Storage Standards and Testing as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers future energy storage technologies as one subject, not present-day batteries alone; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; storage is measured the same way everywhere: energy density, round-trip efficiency, cycle life, and lifetime; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Standard, which is a shared written rule for how a thing is made or tested; Certification, which is the formal record that a product passed its tests; Abuse test, which is a deliberate test that damages a battery to prove it fails safely; Repeatability, which is different laboratories getting the same result from the same test. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future advanced energy storage record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. It answers from this database's own data only, and says plainly when a question goes beyond it.

  • ai-thing.json — the per-database AI spec (the database's own AI, JAH Advanced Energy Storage Librarian).
  • ai-discussion.json — the discussion AI spec, built to discuss Advanced Energy Storage Database with a visitor, record by record.

Both are free downloads, free forever, no sign-in.

8. Demo

Every record in this database carries a working demo program and a boundless generator. The demo for this website is demo.js — run it with node demo.js. It searches the edition's real 24 records and runs a deterministic generator demo (seed 7) over them. The run output is saved in demo-test.txt and shown below, exactly as executed — this demo actually ran.

DATABASE: advanced-energy-storage
EDITION: public
RECORDS: 24
SEARCH "what": 1 hit(s)
  - JAH-ADES-000001 :: What Advanced Energy Storage Is
SEARCH "" (all): 24 record(s)
GENERATOR DEMO (seed 7, 5 samples):
  GEN-1: What Storage
  GEN-2: Record Efficiency
  GEN-3: Materials Pumped
  GEN-4: Hydrogen Sodium
  GEN-5: Safety Solar
TOTAL TITLE CHARS: 672
TITLE CHECKSUM: 3287971940
DEMO OK

Record demos: each of the 24 records carries its own demo program in the database (Python), with an expected output recorded alongside it; each per-file website re-runs its record's demo.
